### Page 1

宏观经济
宏观政策调节力度加大，经济运行积极因素增多
——2026 年 9 月经济数据点评 宏观点评
8 月以来，宏观政策调节力度加大，叠加出口保持高增长，经济运行积
极因素增多。
实际 GDP 增速回升，GDP 平减指数持续正增长。存量政策加快落地，
加上出口保持高增长，三季度实际 GDP 同比增速从 4.3%上升至 4.5%。三季
度名义 GDP 同比增长 6%，GDP 平减指数连续 2 个季度同比正增长。AI 产
业链需求旺盛，加之能源供给扰动，物价有所改善。三季度，CPI 同比上涨
0.73%，涨幅较二季度下降 0.4 个百分点；PPI 同比上涨 4.1%，涨幅较二季
度上升 0.5 个百分点；GDP 平减指数同比增长 1.5%，增幅和二季度基本持
平，连续 2 个季度正增长。
出口高增长加之内需修复，工业生产加速扩张。9 月，工业生产同比增
速 5.5%，较 8 月上升 0.3 个百分点。其原因有三方面。一是高温多雨天气干
扰消退，加之季末效应拉动。二是出口同比增长 36.3%，增速较上月上升 11.3

个百分点，有效支撑工业生产。三是政府债券发行、新型政策性金融工具、
“六张网”等存量政策加快落地，带动相关制造业生产扩张。分行业看，装
备制造业和高技术制造业生产扩张较快。年内，全球信用脉冲仍处在向上阶
段，全球制造业或将保持较高景气度，同时 AI 基建尚未完成，中国出口或
仍保持韧性。加之存量政策加快落地，我们预计工业生产将基本保持稳定。
存量政策效果显现，当月投资同比降幅收窄。9 月，固定资产投资累计
同比下降 7.2%，降幅停止扩大；当月同比降幅从 11%收窄至 7.2%，主要是
因为当月基建投资、制造业投资同比降幅收窄。“六张网”建设加快落实；
政府债券加快发行及使用；新型政策性金融工具实施；下调抵押补充贷款
（PSL）利率 0.25 个百分点，并扩大 PSL 支持领域，另增加多项再贷款额度。
受此影响，基建投资当月同比降幅从 6.9%收窄至 5.4%；制造业投资当月同
比降幅从 6.1%收窄至 1%。
受中秋错位等因素影响，消费增速小幅回升。9 月，社会消费品零售总
额同比增速从 0.4%上升至 1.1%；季调环比增速从-0.16%上升至 0.35%。消

费增速回升主要受以旧换新、中秋错位以及居民收入增速上升的影响。三季
度居民人均可支配收入累计同比增长 5.3%，增速较上季度上升 0.1 个百分点，
连续 2 个季度改善。商品零售总额同比增长 1%，增速上升 0.7 个百分点。
餐饮消费同比增长 2.1%，增速上升 1 个百分点。
信用创造有所改善，保障经济平稳运行。9 月，存量、增量政策加快落
地，政府债券加快发行，信用创造有所修复，新增社融 3.55 万亿元，同比多
增 177 亿元。其中，政府债券净融资 1.5 万亿元，同比多增 3469 亿元。信用
脉冲（12 个月新增社融同比增速）从-10.7%小幅上升至-10.1%。四季度，政
府债券融资或继续同比多增，信用脉冲可能继续回升，社融、M2 同比增速
有望阶段性改善。
 风险提示：新增政策存在不确定性；房地产市场止跌回稳难度超预期；
物价改善内生动力不足；海外需求出现拐点，出口增速下滑；美联储加息路
径不确定性较大。
邢曙光（分析师）
xingshuguang@cctgsc.com.cn

### Page 3

8 月以来，宏观政策调节力度加大，叠加出口保持高增长，经济运行积极因素
增多。三季度，实际 GDP 同比增速从 4.3%上升至 4.5%；物价改善，名义 GDP 同
比增长 6%，GDP 平减指数连续 2 个季度同比正增长。9 月，工业生产同比增速上
升 0.3 个百分点至 5.5%；服务业生产指数同比增速上升 0.7 个百分点至 4.8%；固定
资产投资当月同比降幅从 11%收窄至 7.2%；社会消费品零售总额同比增速从 0.4%
上升至 1.1%；新增社融 3.55 万亿元，同比多增 177 亿元。四季度，政府债券融资
或继续同比多增，信用创造有望持续改善，保障经济平稳运行。
1、 实际 GDP 增速回升，GDP 平减指数持续正增长
存量政策加快落地，加上出口保持高增长，三季度实际 GDP 同比增速从 4.3%
上升至 4.5%。其中，第一产业增加值实际同比增速 3.6%，较二季度下降 0.1 个百
分点；第二产业增加值实际同比增速 3.4%，较二季度上升 0.4 个百分点；第三产业

增加值实际同比增速 5.2%，较二季度上升 0.1 个百分点。
三季度名义 GDP 同比增长 6%，高于实际 GDP 增速，GDP 平减指数连续 2
个季度同比正增长。AI 产业链需求旺盛，加之能源供给扰动，物价有所改善。三
季度，CPI 同比上涨 0.73%，涨幅较二季度下降 0.4 个百分点；PPI 同比上涨 4.1%，
涨幅较二季度上升 0.5 个百分点；GDP 平减指数同比增长 1.5%，增幅和二季度基
本持平，连续 2 个季度正增长。
图 1： 三季度实际 GDP 同比增长 4.5% 图 2： GDP 平减指数连续 2 个季度同比正增长
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
2、 出口高增长加之内需修复，工业生产加速扩张
9 月，工业生产同比增速 5.5%，较 8 月上升 0.3 个百分点。其原因有三方面。
一是高温多雨天气干扰消退，加之季末效应拉动。二是出口同比增长 36.3%，增速
较上月上升 11.3 个百分点，有效支撑工业生产。三是政府债券发行、新型政策性

金融工具、“六张网”等存量政策加快落地，带动相关制造业生产扩张。

### Page 4

图 3： 工业、服务业生产增速回升 图 4： 出口拉动工业生产
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
其中，出口高增长主要有两方面原因。一是全球制造业景气度仍比较高。9 月
全球制造业 PMI 从 52.3%上升至 53%，连续 14 个月维持在 50%以上。美国 ISM 制
造业 PMI 保持在 54.5%的较高水平，欧元区 Markit 制造业 PMI 从 52.7%上升至 52.9%，
日本 Markit 制造业 PMI 54.1%。中国对主要经济体出口保持较高增速，对美国、欧
盟、东盟、日本、韩国、拉美出口同比增速分别从 34.4%、6.6%、30.2%、8.2%、
49.3%、17.5%上升至 39.6%、25.3%、52.3%、15.8%、68.2%、24.7%。劳动密集型
产品出口同比增速从 9.2%上升至 22.8%。二是 AI 产业链需求旺盛。与 AI 产业链
相关的高新技术产品、机电产品出口同比增速较高，分别为 63.7%、43.7%。其中，

集成电路出口同比增速更是高达 130.6%。
图 5： 全球制造业景气度仍较高 图 6： 各类产品出口增速上升
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
分行业看，装备制造业和高技术制造业生产扩张较快。计算机、通信和其他电
子设备制造业，专用设备制造业，铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业，
石油和天然气开采业，通用设备制造业，金属制品业，电气机械及器材制造业，汽
车制造业生产同比增速较高，分别为 16.4%、12.1%、11.7%、9.8%、8.5%、8.2%、
8.1%、6.7%。此外，受供给端约束及产能治理影响，有色金属冶炼及压延加工业生
产同比下降 5.1%。受煤矿停产整顿影响，煤炭开采和洗选业生产同比下降 3.3%。
受房地产投资、基建投资低迷拖累，非金属矿物制品业生产同比下降 2.4%。

### Page 5

年内，全球信用脉冲仍处在向上阶段，全球制造业或将保持较高景气度，同时
AI 基建尚未完成，中国出口或仍保持韧性。加之存量政策加快落地，我们预计工
业生产将基本保持稳定。
图 7： 装备制造业和高技术制造业生产扩张较快
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
3、 存量政策效果显现，当月投资同比降幅收窄
9 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 7.2%，降幅停止扩大；当月同比降幅从 11%
收窄至 7.2%，主要是因为当月基建投资、制造业投资同比降幅收窄。
存量政策效果显现，基建投资、制造业投资降幅收窄。8 月以来，存量政策加
快落地。“六张网”建设加快落实；政府债券加快发行及使用，9 月政府债券净融
资 1.5 万亿元，同比多增 3469 亿元；新型政策性金融工具实施，资金规模达 8000
亿元，较上年增加 3000 亿元；下调抵押补充贷款（PSL）利率 0.25 个百分点，并
扩大 PSL 支持领域，另增加多项再贷款额度。受此影响，基建投资当月同比降幅
从 6.9%收窄至 5.4%；制造业投资当月同比降幅从 6.1%收窄至 1%。设备工器具购

置投资累计同比增速从 9.3%上升至 9.7%。铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备
制造业，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业，电气机械及器材制造业，通用设备
制造业投资分别累计同比增长 15.8%、9.6%、1.2%、1.1%。

### Page 6

图 8： 当月投资同比降幅收窄 图 9： 当月基建、制造业投资同比降幅收窄
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
图 10： 工业产能利用率仍处于低位 图 11： 设备工器具购置投资增速较高
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
房地产市场止跌回稳难度较大。虽然房地产市场需求结构性改善，但整体商品
房销售面积累计同比下降 13.2%，降幅扩大 1.1 个百分点。整体库存压力较大，房
企资金压力仍存，房地产投资累计同比下降 20.6%，降幅扩大 0.7 个百分点。与此
同时，房屋新开工面积累计同比下降 24.9%，降幅扩大 0.1 个百分点；房屋施工面
积累计同比下降 12.8%，降幅仍较大。
房价分化。一线城市房地产价格有所改善，但持续性仍待观察。一线城市二手
住宅销售价格环比上涨 0.2%，涨幅扩大 0.1 个百分点，连续 7 个月上涨。二线城市
二手住宅销售价格环比下降 0.2%，降幅收窄 0.1 个百分点。三线城市二手住宅销售

价格环比下降 0.3%，降幅持平上月。

### Page 7

图 12： 房地产投资降幅扩大 图 13： 房价分化
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
4、 受中秋错位等因素影响，消费增速小幅回升
9 月，社会消费品零售总额同比增速从 0.4%上升至 1.1%；季调环比增速从
-0.16%上升至 0.35%。消费增速回升主要受以旧换新、中秋错位以及居民收入增速
上升的影响。三季度居民人均可支配收入累计同比增长 5.3%，增速较上季度上升
0.1 个百分点，连续 2 个季度改善。
商品零售总额同比增长 1%，增速上升 0.7 个百分点。通讯器材消费同比增长
31.1%，文化办公用品消费同比增长 7.9%，主要是因为以旧换新政策优化实施，加
上新一批补贴资金下发，以及新学期开学需求释放。受中秋错位影响，粮油、食品，
饮料，烟酒消费同比增速分别上升 2.7、0.1、1.3 个百分点，至 6.7%、5%、13.8%。
受油价上涨影响，石油及制品消费同比增速上升 6.2 个百分点至-0.8%。受换季影响，
服装鞋帽针纺织品消费同比增速上升 1.7 个百分点至 1.2%。此外，汽车消费补贴退

坡加之高基数，汽车消费同比下降 21.2%。受房地产市场拖累，家具、建筑及装潢
材料消费分别同比下降 3%、9.2%。
与此同时，受中秋错位影响，服务消费增速有所回升。餐饮消费当月同比增长
2.1%，增速上升 1 个百分点。服务零售额累计同比增长 5%，增速上升 0.1 个百分
点。
服务业生产指数同比增速上升 0.7 个百分点至 4.8%。其中，信息传输、软件和
信息技术服务业，租赁和商务服务业，交通运输、仓储和邮政业生产同比增速分别
上升 0.2、0.5、0.6 个百分点至 9.8%、9.5%、5.4%。

### Page 8

图 14： 居民收入增速上升，消费边际改善
资料来源：Wind，诚通证券研究所
图 15： 通讯器材等消费增速较高

5、 信用创造有所改善，保障经济平稳运行
9 月，存量、增量政策加快落地，政府债券加快发行，信用创造有所修复，新
增社融 3.55 万亿元，同比多增 177 亿元。其中，政府债券净融资 1.5 万亿元，同比
多增 3469 亿元。与此同时，民间融资、信贷仍待进一步改善。新增贷款 1.08 万亿
元，同比少增 2100 亿元。其中，新增居民中长期贷款 874 亿元，同比少增 1626 亿
元；新增企业中长期贷款 8900 亿元，同比少增 200 亿元。
虽然社融余额、M2 同比增速分别从 7.2%、7.5%继续下降至 7.1%、7.2%，不
过领先指标信用脉冲（12 个月新增社融同比增速）从-10.7%小幅上升至-10.1%。四
季度，政府债券融资或继续同比多增，信用脉冲可能继续回升，社融、M2 同比增
速有望阶段性改善。但由于全年广义赤字规模基本持平上年、广义赤字率可能下降，

Chenbro  | Asia Pacific
September Sales +1% MoM/
+14% YoY
In this report, we focus on Chenbro's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September  sales were NT$2,476mn (+1% MoM/+14% YoY).
• 3Q26 sales came in at NT$7,395mn (-5% QoQ/+31% YoY), short of our 3Q26
estimate at NT$8,368mn (+7% QoQ/+48% YoY) and the consensus at NT
$8,552mn (+9% QoQ/+51% YoY).
Our view:
• 3Q26 revenue fell slightly short of the buyside expectation of roughly flat

QoQ, in our view, and 4Q26 should be flat to up slightly  QoQ.
• We think near-term sentiment will remain lackluster amid the temporary
hiccup resulting from supply-chain management issues and its customer's
model transition. That said, demand from its GPU/ASIC server clusters and
general servers should remain strong, driving  orders for its chassis and rack
offerings.
• Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chenbro (8210.TW)
Base case, derived from 27x 2027e P/E. We view our target multiple as justified by the 33%
earnings CAGR we see for 2025-28, which implies a PEG of 0.8. This is in line the average of
0.8 over 2014-24, when the stock was trading at an average P/E of 12x for a 15% earnings
CAGR.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger AI capex outlook
n Larger market share gains within existing customers

n Faster penetration into new customers
n Higher entry barrier among peers that leads to higher ASP/profitability
n Better macro outlook
Risks to Downside
n Softer AI capex
n Less share gains within existing customers
n Slower penetration into new customers
n Intensifying competition leading to lower ASP/profitability
n Continuous macro uncertainties

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Genius Electronic Optical
Private placement with 3M expands its optical
connectivity opportunity
Genius approved a private placement with 3M
On 15th Oct, Genius's board approved a private placement to 3M UK Limited for
5.54mn shares at NT$869.6 per share, an 18.3% discount to the 14th Oct closing price
NT$1,065, representing NT$4.8bn investment and 4.68% stake in Genius. Proceeds
from the transaction will fund technology development, working capital, and bank loan

repayment. The two companies also announced a strategic partnership to develop
optical communication for AI data center and HPC. We believe this announcement is a
positive validation for Genius working with a tier one supplier in optical connectivity to
provide its precision components for advanced optics.
3M EBO technology could open up a US$100mn TAM for Genius
3M has been developing its EBO (expanded beam optical) technology since 2019 with
commercialization in late 2024 and deployment for Microsoft Azure data centers in July

2026. EBO is an upgrade to MPO connectors at the ends of fiber optics cables.  The
EBO is a lensed, non-contact ferrule that uses a prism lens to expand and collimate a
light beam to send across fiber before refocusing into a receiving fiber, improving dust
tolerance to cut cleaning steps and cable inspection times 85%. The technology was
supported by the MSA launched in May 2026, co-chaired by Oracle, with Meta, Senko
and Molex among the members for scale-out cabling. We saw opportunity opened for

Genius on the EBO ferrule, a small molded plastic part with a row of micro-lenses to
widen the light beam plus an anti reflection coating. This could open up a US$100mn
market TAM for Genius based on 4-12 ferrules per GPU (2-3 connections x 2-4 plugs per
connection) x 20mn accelerators x US$1 for the lens assembly.
Validation for its optical solution push with potential hyperscaler exposure
We believe the scale of placement at NT$4.8bn suggests scope to expand into CPO

where it could improve the MT ferrule used as the detachable plug end of the FAU to
hold the fiber in position when it is connected to a switch adapter or removable cable.
Genius  collaboration with 3M can bring the company closer to the end demand with its
direct partnership with Microsoft and bring revenue contribution forward to 2027,
ahead of Genius CPO ramp in 2028E. For CPO, we still see potential to reach 10% of the
TAM supplying MT ferrule, prism and V-groove.

Reiterate Buy with PT at NT$1,250
Our recent update on Genius had raised the PT from NT$700 to NT$1,250 reflecting the
company's potential CPO market with its progress sampling three precision components
inside the fiber array unit (FAU) targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.
The collaboration strengthens our confidence on Genius capability within the optical
connectivity industry, and reiterate our Buy on the compony.

Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model.
Genius' growth is highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by
volatility in handset demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their

camera design. Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of
new technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

水冷機櫃與機殼本業同步走升 
 2Q26 本業穩健成長 ，EPS 1.41 元：晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，
年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至 20.5%，主要是因客戶
Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟階段，客戶
持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。 
 預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26 開始爬升：展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年
增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為傳統旺季，加上
VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，出貨
量將逐步上升 ， 隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，
預期機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案
有 8 至 9 成聚焦機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11
系統整合業務營收將進一步提升。 
 維持買進，目標價 140 元：投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機
殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進一步成長，且公司已成功切入
Meta 供應鏈，並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認證，可直接承接
Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟銘電
水冷相關業務比重持續提升 ， 使2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以
上 ， 投顧預估2026 年 EPS 6.13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元 ， 維持買進 ，
目標價 140 元。

個股報告

晟銘電 為伺服器機殼大廠，目前
切入 IT 機櫃、水冷機櫃、水冷搭
配的分歧管 
 晟銘電子科技股份有限公司成立於 1976 年 6 月 ， 為台灣伺服器機殼OEM/ODM
製造商， 公司主要銷售電腦機殼及伺服器機殼 ，其用途為桌上型電腦及伺服器等
各項組件之安裝防護設備 ， 近年來公司積極跨足散熱領域並研發設計出液冷散熱
的浸沒式散熱產品。於 2024 年公司推出多款液冷機櫃及液冷散熱系統。晟銘電
提供全球客戶迅速且彈性的 OEM 及 ODM 生產服務。2026 年 1~8 月主要產品
營收占比，伺服器 73%、L11 系統整合 18%、Tooling 6%、DT 3%。

Exhibit 3: 產品組合

Sources : Company、MasterLink

2Q26 本業穩健成長，EPS 1.41
元 
 晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至
20.5%，主要是因客戶 Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟
階段，客戶持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。

預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26
開始爬升 
 展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為
傳統旺季，加上 VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，
出貨量將逐步上升，隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，預期
機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案有 8 至 9 成聚焦
機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11 系統整合業務營收將進一步
提升。

今年前八月 L11 系統組裝營收占比已達 18％ ， 而明年L11 系統組裝營收貢獻還
會持續提升。公司在機櫃產品線已相當完整，還可提供配套的水冷配件，機箱
基本上是跟隨著輝達的腳步走 ， 包括GB300、VR200 等標準品機箱都已經開好
模具。海外產能布局，公司目前生產基地仍以中國大陸為主，其中東莞為主要
生產基地，另於寧波設有產能，產品以機殼、機櫃為主，東莞廠自動烤漆線預
計今年底前完成，一天可以完成60 個機櫃烤漆；台灣中壢則主要負責打樣及水
冷產品研發，部分零組件仍由中國生產後運送至中壢進行後段組裝。泰國一期
廠已完成建置並具備生產條件 ， 二期則持續進行設備調校 ， 主要導入烤漆產線，
預計 4Q26 開始試產，後續待客戶完成稽核及認證後逐步量產。泰國二期的烤

漆設備規格除可支援機殼外，亦可延伸至機櫃產品，有助未來將部分機櫃製程

 投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進
一步成長 ， 且公司已成功切入Meta 供應鏈 ， 並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認
證，可直接承接Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟
銘電水冷相關業務比重持續提升，使 2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以上，
投顧預估 2026 年 EPS 6. 13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元，維持買進，目標價 140
元。(15x2027EPS)

風險提示

1) 客戶機箱導入狀況。 
2) L11 系統組裝發展狀況。

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 8 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

2026.09.21   
LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속 Company Brief

3Q26 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 영업이익 시장 기대치 상회 전망 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 8.8조원(+45% YoY, + 16% QoQ), 영업이익 3,690억원(-
39% YoY, +226% QoQ)을 기록하며 원/달러 환율 하락으로 매출은 시장 기대치를 
소폭 하회하겠으나 영업이익은 상회할 것으로 전망된다. AMPC 는 미국 ESS 생산량 
증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 힘입어 전분기 대비 약 57% 증가한 3,790억원으로 
추정된다. 미국 상호관세 위법 판결에 따른 관세 환급금이나 완성차 업체들의 최소 
구매 물량 미달 보상금 반영 여부에 따라 추가적인 이익 개선 가능성도 존재한다. ESS 
부문은 전분기에서 이연된 물량의 출하가 본격화되면서 3Q26 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
38% 증가할 것으로 예상된다. 특히 미국 ESS 생산라인의 가동 안정화와 AMPC 수혜 
확대에 힘입어 수익성도 점진적으로 개선될 것으로 보인다. 자동차전지 부문도 
3Q26부터 회복세가 강화될 것으로 예상된다. 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 
재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 큰 폭으로 증가하고, 고정비 부담 완화로 적자폭도 
뚜렷하게 축소될 전망이다. 소형전지 부문은 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 
안정적인 출하 증가로 매출 회복과 수익성 방어가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 
4Q26 대규모 ESS 신규 수주 기대 
전세계 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 힘입어 빠르게 
성장하고 있다. 시장조사기관 SNE리서치에 따르면 1H26 전세계 ESS 배터리 
출하량은 461GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 71% 증가했다. 이 중 북미 출하량은 
76GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 83% 증가했으며, 분기별로는 1Q26 35GWh(+91% 
YoY), 2Q26 41GWh(+76% YoY) 를 기록했다. 통상 하반기에 출하가 집중되는 
계절성을 감안하면 올해 북미 ESS 배터리 출하량은 전년 대비 약 70% 증가한 
170~180GWh에 이를 것으로 전망된다. 연초 동사가 예상한 올해 북미 ESS 수요 
성장률 10% 초반 수준을 크게 상회할 가능성이 높은 상황이다. 올해 동사의 ESS 
신규 수주 목표는 약 90GWh이며, 현재까지 약 30GWh의 수주를 확정한 것으로 
파악된다. 이에 시장 일각에서는 연간 수주 목표 달성 여부에 대한 우려가 제기되고 
있다. 그러나 당사가 파악한 바에 따르면 대규모 ESS 신규 수주가 4Q26에 집중될 
것으로 예상된다. 특히 북미 ESS 수요의 가파른 증가세와 투자세액공제(48E)에 
연계된 PFE 공급망 규제 시행은 동사의 수주 확보에 우호적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 향후 국내 배터리 셀 업체들의 
북미 ESS 신규 수주가 지속적으로 확대될 전망이다. 미국 ESS 시장에서는 신재생 
에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 따른 수요 증가, 중국 공급망에 대한 규제 
강화가 맞물리면서 국내 업체들에 우호적인 사업 환경이 조성되고 있다. 동사 역시 
ESS 중심의 생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화될 경우 매출 성장과 함께 고정비 
부담 완화, AMPC 수혜 확대에 따른 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 이를 바탕으로 동사의 
영업이익은 2026년 약 0.8조원에서 2028년 약 5.1조원으로 가파르게 증가할 것으로 
전망된다. ESS의 역할이 신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AIDC의 안정적인 
전력 공급을 지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 
이에 따라 동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 
산업의 성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다고 판단된다. 업종 내에서 실적 
개선 가시성과 정책 수혜가 뚜렷한 동사에 대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다. 
Buy (Maintain)

당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

### Page 1

2026 年三季度以来，全球市场巨震：
股市⽅⾯， AI 科技股遭遇年内第⼆轮剧烈调整。 10 ⽉初 OpenAI 披露年化收⼊接近 500 亿美
元，远低于市场预期的 700 亿美元，引发 AI 概念股⼤规模抛售。费城半导体指数连跌 8 个交易
⽇，创 2008 年以来最⻓连跌纪录；纳指单周累计下跌 4.48% ，为 6 ⽉以来最⼤单周跌幅；英伟
达 9 个交易⽇市值较⾼点蒸发约 0.6 万亿美元。韩国股市由上半年全球最强市场转为重灾区，
KOSPI 指数 10 ⽉ 6 ⽇以来连跌 5 ⽇、累计跌幅近 15% ，创 8 ⽉以来新低。A 股科技成⻓显著回
调，创业板指从 6 ⽉ 9 ⽇的 3961 点回落⾄ 10 ⽉中旬的 2963 点，上证指数⼀度失守 3700 点，⽩
酒、银⾏等红利消费板块逆势⾛强。港股恒⽣科技指数年内跌幅仍超 25% ，点位接近 2024
年 “9·24” ⾏情区间。
债市⽅⾯，美债收益率全线飙升。美联储 9 ⽉ 16 ⽇宣布加息 25 个基点⾄ 3.75%-4.00% ，为

2023 年 7 ⽉以来⾸次加息，点阵图显⽰年内仍有⼀次加息。 10 年期美债收益率从 7 ⽉中旬的
4.55% 左右⼀路上⾏， 10 ⽉ 7 ⽇升⾄ 5.365% ，创 2002 年 4 ⽉以来新⾼； 30 年期美债收益率升
⾄ 5.732% 。中债则延续低位震荡， 10 年期国债收益率在 1.68% 附近，中美 10 年期利差倒挂约
356 个基点，处于历史⾼位区间。
⼤宗商品⽅⾯，中东冲突再度升级，美伊在霍尔⽊兹海峡附近冲突加剧，胡塞武装袭击沙特
能源设施，油价重回 100 美元 / 桶上⽅， 10 ⽉中旬 WTI 原油单周再涨超 10% 。贵⾦属则⾼位震
荡：⾦价⾃ 2026 年 1 ⽉创历史新⾼后最⼤回撤约 25% ， 7 ⽉⼀度回落⾄ 4000 美元 / 盎司附近， 8
⽉反弹⾄ 4600 美元 / 盎司， 10 ⽉以来⼜回落⾄ 4100 ⾄ 4200 美元 / 盎司区间，年内由涨转跌。
美债、美股、⻩⾦、 A 股的定价逻辑都在剧烈重构，踏准节奏的投资者有效规避了回撤，追⾼热

⻔板块的则损失惨重。为了帮⼤家看清 2026 年四季度⼤类资产轮动与⻛向变化，我们邀请了百
亿私募敦和资管⾸席经济学家徐⼩庆，于 10 ⽉ 25 ⽇周⽇晚 19:00 担任 Alpha 线上闭⻔私享会主讲
嘉宾，分享他对四季度宏观⼤类资产配置⻛向的最新研判。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

10 ⽉ 25 ⽇周⽇晚上 19 点，百亿私募基⾦敦和资管的⾸席经济学家徐⼩庆主讲的Alpha 线上闭⻔私
享会，将会分享他最近研究关于股票、债券、商品、外汇、⻩⾦等⼤类资产的最新变化，研判
当前宏观⾛势与 2026 下半资产配置⽅向，看清背后的配置逻辑与关键时机，助⼒个⼈的关键决
策。
这是徐⼩庆今年第三次做客 Alpha 闭⻔会。
2 ⽉的分享：在⻩⾦⽩银暴跌后，他提出贵⾦属将从单边上涨进⼊震荡格局，并提⽰美股居⺠
参与度已超互联⽹泡沫时期、市场脆弱性显著上升。
7 ⽉的分享：他将半导体与年初的⽩银⾛势类⽐，提⽰ AI 科技股可能正在构筑阶段性顶部，同
时判断⻩⾦进⼊⾼位震荡、 4000 美元 / 盎司以下具备配置价值。
此后数⽉的市场演绎印证了上述多项判断。站在四季度的新起点，美债利率为何突破 5% ⼤幅上
⾏，何时迎来拐点？ A 股⻛格切换是否已经开启？⻩⾦何时王者归来？这些议题都将在本次分享
中得到详细展开。
徐⼩庆曾任中⾦公司⾸席债券分析师、董事总经理，带队 4 次当选新财富固定收益研究 “ 最佳分

析师 ” 第⼀名，连续 10 年⼊围前三名。在⼗年间持续获得掌管数万亿资产的⾦融机构集体投票⽀
持，徐⼩庆是中国 “ 最懂利率 “ 的⼏个⼈之⼀。2013 年，徐⼩庆加⼊百亿私募基⾦敦和资管担任
⾸席经济学家、宏观策略总监，并担任敦和宏观对冲系列基⾦的投资经理。
20 多年的从业经历⾥，从卖⽅研究最佳分析师到百亿私募的买⽅投资⼈，徐⼩庆将对中国利率
变动影响资产轮动的前沿研究，⽤于投资实践指引资产配置。早在 2018 年，华尔街⻅闻就邀请
徐⼩庆打造⻅闻⼤师课《⼗年利率⼼法》。在课程中，徐⼩庆⽴⾜中国市场，总结出⼀套⾏之有
效的中国特⾊ “ 利率为锚 ” 的资产轮动与配置⽅法。这是他从业 20 多年间，亲历中国债券市场 5 轮
⽜熊轮动，对中国利率周期影响资产轮动进⾏系统跟踪研究的成果。这⻔课程⽬前已获得上万
名⽤⼾付费订阅，⼀直是⽤⼾评价最⾼和回看复习最多的课程。
每次 Alpha 闭⻔分享也特别安排了 30 分钟的问答互动环节，参加的会员朋友可以针对⾃⼰最关⼼
的话题，获得嘉宾的答疑解惑。对闭⻔分享活动感兴趣的朋友，可以点击下⽅活动海报报名加

⼊ Alpha 线上闭⻔私享会会员，⼀起来听听他对市场的最新洞察。

Belgium’s current failure to agree on a three-year €10bn
savings package is not yet a political crisis, but it is a missed
opportunity. A deal on the 2027 budget remains possible, yet
delaying the harder choices risks weakening the country’s
ﬁscal credibility as the 2029 elections draw closer
Philippe Ledent
Belgian PM Bart De Wever
This was supposed to be a decisive week for Belgium’s public
ﬁnances. The prime minister was due to deliver his general policy

statement to parliament on Tuesday, outlining a budget
agreement among the governing parties for the next three years,
up to the 2029 elections. In addition to measures already adopted,
the government is seeking €10bn in recurring savings, phased in
over the next three years.
A new timetable for negotiations
For now, however, the budget talks have failed to produce an
agreement. A central element of the package was a one-

percentage-point increase in the standard VAT rate, from 21% to
22%, but one coalition party ﬁrmly opposed the proposal. The
general policy statement therefore had to be postponed. Under
Prime Minister Bart De Wever's revised timetable, the immediate
priority is to secure agreement on the 2027 budget, including €4bn
in savings.
Ideally, a deal would be reached by the end of October, allowing
parliament to debate and approve the legislation before year-end.

This would avoid recourse to the system of provisional twelfths,
under which the previous year’s budget is rolled over on a monthly
basis to keep public services running. The PM has made clear that
he wants to avoid such an outcome at all costs. He is even prepared
to push through legislation that would trigger automatic spending
cuts of €3-4bn from January 2027 if no budget is agreed by year-
end.
In a second phase, negotiations are expected to resume with the

aim of ﬁnding a further €6bn in savings across the 2028 and 2029
budgets. This stage will be crucial for Belgium’s medium-term ﬁscal
trajectory. Postponing those discussions until next year is a risk. By
then, the federal and regional elections scheduled for spring 2029
would be closer, and the wide political diﬀerences within the current
coalition would make agreement on additional measures more
diﬃcult.
A chance to stand apart from France
There is no political crisis at this stage. With a package of measures,

Belgium should still be able to adopt a 2027 budget that brings the
deﬁcit below 5% of GDP. Even so, failure to reach a swift and
comprehensive agreement on the full €10bn package is a missed
opportunity for the federal government to show ﬁnancial markets
that Belgium is not France.
On headline indicators – public deﬁcits, debt and even economic
growth – little separates the two countries. France’s diﬃculty,
however, lies in its inability to set and pass a budget, compounded

by the political uncertainty surrounding the April 2027 presidential
election. Belgium, by contrast, has a governing majority capable of

Document 254: Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 mi

Source PDF: all report pdf/Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13028181     1

Economy 
Business Checkpoint: Looking into the logistics 
15 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Small and mid-sized businesses entered Q3 in relatively solid financial condition, with profitability growth up across both 
groups, according to Bank of America business account data. Nearly half of estimated tariff payments had been refunded by 
September, though the share of these refunds relative to revenue has peaked. 
• In Bank of America business payments data, logistics spending growth per client was up four times as much for small than mid-
sized firms. Additionally, distribution and inventory transactions growth per client has outpaced spending since the spring of 
2025. 
• Growing transportation bottlenecks are re-emerging ahead of the holiday season. Trucking capacity has tightened as driver 
availability remains constrained, according to BofA Global Research. This has pushed freight rates higher and driven strong 
transportation sector payroll growth, particularly among mid-sized businesses, according to Bank of America payments data.

Business Checkpoint is a new, regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It provides a real-time view of the financial health, opportunities 
and challenges facing small and mid-sized businesses across the US economy, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America's proprietary 
data. By analyzing activity among both small and mid-sized firms, Business Checkpoint offers timely insights into business profitability, hiring, 
investment and spending trends. Such data is not intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, 
financial condition or performance of Bank of America. 
Businesses end Q3 in fair shape 
While profitability growth remained positive for both small and mid-sized firms in the third quarter, small firms outpaced their 
mid-sized counterparts for the second consecutive quarter (Exhibit 2). Still, there’s been a notable reversal in the profitability of 
mid-sized businesses, growing this quarter after declining slightly for the past two.  
The continued rollout of tariff refunds is serving as a tailwind to some firms' cash positions. That is particularly true for small 
firms. According to Bank of America account data, by September approximately 47% of estimated International Emergency 
Economic Powers Act-related tariff payments had been refunded to companies with less than $50 million in annual revenue.

in annual revenue peaked in June

According to Bank of America business payments data, the percentage point difference in tariff refunds as a share of revenue 
for firms with less than $50 million in annual revenue spiked in June but dropped to nearly zero in September. At the same time, 
the percentage point difference in tariff payments as a share of expenses began rising again relative to the 2023 average 
(Exhibit 1). This suggests that while tariffs continue to affect companies, the refund program has provided temporary relief for 
some businesses.  
Wholesale trade along with transportation and warehousing posted solid profitability growth across business size 
However, the benefits appear uneven. Such refunds have boosted cash flow, but they have not fully insulated firms from broader 
cost pressures.  
Looking across industries, mid-sized construction and wholesale firms had the strongest profitability growth in September 
(Exhibit 3). Notably, transportation and warehousing, as well as wholesale trade were among the few sectors showing positive 
profitability growth for both small and mid-sized firms last month. 
The persistence of logistics, freight and inventory-related cost pressures may help explain why profitability gains have remained 
relatively concentrated in transportation and wholesale trade. However, these sectors also have a higher exposure to rising gas 
prices within their margins (read more on this in the August Small Business Checkpoint). 
Exhibit 3: For both small and mid-sized businesses, profitability growth was positive in transportation and warehousing as well as wholesale trade 
in September 
Profitability growth by sector and business size in September (YoY%, six-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The cost of maintaining supply chains is rising 
In fact, the prices paid component of the September services reading from the Institute for Supply Management indicates still-
building price pressure for firms (Exhibit 4). This marked the highest reading since July 2022.   
Using Bank of America internal data, we identified payments to logistics and freight companies to assess a key part of supply 
chain costs. In June, logistics and freight payments’ growth per client from small businesses grew nearly 30%, though has 
moderated to 12.7% YoY in September (Exhibit 5). Meanwhile, mid-sized firms’ payments growth in this category was up just 
3.2% YoY in September. 
The divergence suggests logistics costs may be disproportionately affecting smaller firms, who are typically less capable of 
absorbing rising costs than larger firms. Larger companies also often benefit from dedicated logistics contracts and greater 
purchasing power, while smaller ones may be more exposed to spot-market transportation costs and fluctuations in freight 
pricing.

Evidence of more moving parts in companies’ supply chains 
Yet there is increasing evidence that tariffs and geopolitics are adding complexity to small and mid-sized companies’ supply 
chains. 
In Bank of America payments data, average distribution and inventory transaction growth per business client has increased 
steadily since the spring of 2025 despite spending growth in this category falling in September (Exhibit 6). Rising transaction 
growth alongside softer spending growth suggests businesses may be engaging with more suppliers, shipments or inventory 
movements even as overall procurement outlays moderates.  
Furthermore, in May 2026, the Global Supply Chain Pressure Index reached its highest reading since the summer of 2022 and has 
continued to remain elevated into October (Exhibit 7). This further emphasizes that transportation bottlenecks, delays and 
procurement challenges are becoming more common globally. While current readings remain below the extreme levels observed 
during the pandemic, the recent move higher indicates supply chain normalization may be reversing.

The trials of trucking  
As of October 1, BofA Global Research's proprietary bi-weekly Truckload Demand Indicator (see Methodology) remained below 
its historical growth signal (Exhibit 9) and the Truck Capacity Indicator, which reflects shippers' views of available capacity, 
tightened significantly, falling from 39.2 to 28.4 (Exhibit 8). This suggests shippers are seeing fewer trucks available relative to 
demand than in the previous survey. 
According to BofA Global Research, the current trucking upcycle is being driven by constrained supply rather than a significant 
improvement in freight demand. While any positive movement in demand is an important indicator as the industry moves into 
peak shipping season between late September and early December, tighter capacity appears to be the dominant force 
supporting freight rates. 
Exhibit 8: Shippers’ view of available capacity has fallen 
significantly as of October 1, 2026 
BofA Truckload Capacity Indicator by year (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 9: The Demand Indicator has been sub-60 for the past two 
months after 4.5 months above 
BofA Truckload Demand Indicator (bi-weekly)

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The reduction in capacity reflects several factors, including stricter commercial driver's license (CDL) and training requirements, 
increased compliance and enforcement measures, and other regulatory changes that have reduced the pool of qualified drivers. 
This has also likely driven some wage inflation within the industry. According to Bank of America payments data, transportation 
and warehousing payroll growth per client has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses (Exhibit 10).  
Having to pay higher wages suggests labor shortages remain a challenge in the sector. Firms may increasingly need to offer 
higher compensation to attract and retain workers as trucking capacity tightens. In turn, such capacity puts upward pressure on 
freight rates, particularly in the spot market where small businesses may be more impacted. 
Exhibit 10: Transportation and warehousing payroll growth has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses

Logistics payments tend to increase in the second half of the year 
September marks a key run-up to the holiday season (read more on signs of holiday spending in the October Consumer 
Checkpoint), with spending on ocean shipping, containers, and other transportation services steadily accelerating ahead of the 
peak shopping period.  
In fact, over the past two years, the month of November has seen ocean shipping/container spending volumes well above their 
2023-2025 monthly average (Exhibit 11). The seasonal pattern likely reflects inventory restocking and holiday-related shipping 
activity. Ocean shipping volumes typically accelerate during the fall months as businesses prepare for year-end demand. 
Exhibit 11: Ocean shipping/container spending volumes were well above their 2023-2025 monthly average in November 
Logistics and freight average payments by category (monthly, indexed, 100 = monthly 2023-2025 average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Distributors and inventory payments’ growth fell ahead of holiday season 
Changes in logistics and freight spending often provide an early signal of inventory trends, as distributors account for a large 
share of supply chain-related spending (Exhibit 12). 
The sharp moderation in distribution and inventory spending growth per client for both small and mid-sized businesses indicates 
that much of the large-scale inventory accumulation seen earlier in 2025 has already occurred (Exhibit 13). In other words, 
businesses appear to be replenishing inventories for seasonal demand rather than significantly expanding stockpiles as they did 
earlier in the year. 
Overall, businesses seem to be entering the holiday season in relatively stable financial condition, supported by tariff refunds 
and positive profitability growth. However, rising logistics costs, tighter trucking capacity and elevated supply chain pressures 
suggest operating conditions are becoming more challenging.

I NSTI TUTE

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business and/or Business Banking clients with a deposit account or a 
Small Business credit card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of 
America per Business client data represents activity spending from active Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card and at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and are 
generally defined as under $5mm in annual sales revenue. Mid-sized businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America 
and are generally defined as $5mm-$50mm in annual sales revenue. 
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Revenue tiers are determined by the combination of following factors: 1) stated revenue on small businesses credit applications, 2) actual 
account inflow into Bank of America Deposit Accounts, and 3) third party revenue estimation.  
The alternative hiring indicator consists of payments from Bank of America business clients to business-focused hiring firms which include both 
direct deposits through Automated Clearing House (ACH) and payments via credit and debit cards. 
Supply chain-related spending is comprised of payments to logistics, freight, distribution, inventory, delivery service, and trucking companies. 
These are identified from merchant names and MCC codes. 
BofA Global Research’s trucking indicators are a bi-weekly diffusion-style survey of shipper demand expectations over the next 0–3 months, 
built from responses from surveyed shippers. 
Data regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

KQKD 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ

Mạng bay Trung Đông phục hồi hỗ 
trợ sản lượng SCS từ 2H2026

Long Thành tạo áp lực lên sản lượng 
và biên lợi nhuận từ 2027, triển vọng 
dài hạn phụ thuộc khả năng tham 
gia vận hành nhà ga mới

Cổ tức 2026 dự kiến ở mức 5,000 
VND/cổ phiếu

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 50,700 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, SCS ghi nhận doanh thu 279 tỷ VND (-4% yoy), LNST đạt 188 tỷ VND (đi 
ngang svck). Lũy kế 1H2026, doanh thu đạt 551 tỷ VND (-1% yoy), LNST đạt 361 tỷ 
VND (+0.5% yoy), trong đó thu nhập tài chính tăng mạnh bù đắp phần lớn sự suy 
giảm lợi nhuận từ hoạt động khai thác nhà ga.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng mùa cao điểm hàng hóa cuối năm và mạng bay Trung Đông 
phục hồi sẽ hỗ trợ sản lượng SCS trong những tháng cuối năm. Tuy nhiên, mức độ 
cải thiện còn hạn chế do nhu cầu vận tải trên các tuyến Trung Đông – châu Á và 
châu Âu – Trung Đông vẫn suy yếu. Sản lượng hàng quốc tế/nội địa năm 2026 dự 
kiến đạt 209/52 nghìn tấn (-7%/-4% yoy).

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sau khi các hạ tầng kết nối với sân bay Long Thành được hoàn 
thiện, tỷ lệ phân bổ chuyến bay quốc tế giữa Long Thành và Tân Sơn Nhất sẽ dao 
động quanh khoảng 75%/25%. Tỷ trọng phân bổ hàng quốc tế tại TP. Hồ Chí Minh 
qua Long Thành 2027/2028/2029 dự kiến sẽ đạt 10%/35%/55%.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng SCS tiếp tục duy trì cổ tức tiền mặt cao nhờ khả năng tạo dòng 
tiền ổn định và quy mô tiền gửi lớn. Cổ tức năm 2026 dự kiến đạt 5,000 VND/cổ 
phiếu, trước khi duy trì ở mức 3,000 VND/cổ phiếu/năm từ 2027 nhằm tích lũy 
nguồn lực cho các dự án mới.

Dựa trên triển vọng kinh doanh và kết quả định giá, chúng tôi đưa ra khuyến nghị 
TRUNG LẬP với cổ phiếu SCS, mức giá mục tiêu 50,700 VND/cổ phiếu.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh

CTCP Dịch vụ hàng hóa Sài Gòn được thành 
lập năm 2008, hiện là đơn vị khai thác nhà ga 
hàng hóa hàng không lớn thứ 2 tại cảng 
hàng không quốc tế (HKQT) Tân Sơn Nhất 
với thị phần khoảng 50%. Các cổ đông lớn 
của SCS bao gồm CTCP Gemadept (33.4%) 
và Tổng Công ty Cảng Hàng không Việt Nam 
(13.6%). 
Hoạt động khai thác nhà ga là mảng kinh 
doanh chính của SCS. Hiện SCS đang tiếp 
tục mua sắm trang thiết bị nâng công suất 
xử lý hàng lên 350,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Sản lượng hàng quốc tế/nội địa năm 2026 dự kiến đạt 209/52 nghìn tấn (-7%/-4% yoy) dù đã được hỗ 
trợ bởi mạng bay Trung Đông phục hồi và mùa cao điểm cuối năm 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Thông lượng hàng hóa quốc tế qua SCS bắt đầu giảm từ 2027 với 10%/35%/55%/70% hàng quốc tế 
Tân Sơn Nhất phân bổ sang cho L TA vào 2027/2028/2029/2030, trước khi bắt đầu tăng trưởng trở lại từ 
2031. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
SCS tiếp tục duy trì chính sách cổ tức tiền mặt ổn định nhờ nền tảng tài chính lành mạnh, cổ tức 2026 
dự kiến đạt 5,000 VND/cổ phiếu và duy trì ở mức 3,000 VND/cổ phiếu/năm từ 2027.

Biểu đồ 4. Cơ cấu thông lượng hàng SCS (tấn)  Biểu đồ 5. Giá dịch vụ và biên LNG hoạt động khai thác nhà ga

Nguồn: SCS  Nguồn: SCS

Mạng bay Trung Đông phục hồi hỗ 
trợ sản lượng SCS trong 2H2026, 
song mức độ cải thiện còn hạn chế 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng hàng hóa của SCS sẽ cải thiện trong những tháng 
cuối năm 2026 nhờ mạng bay Trung Đông dần được khôi phục, đặc biệt từ 
Qatar Airways, cùng nhu cầu vận tải hàng hóa tăng trong mùa cao điểm cuối 
năm. Tuy nhiên, mức độ phục hồi dự kiến còn hạn chế khi nhu cầu vận tải trên 
các tuyến Trung Đông – Châu Á và Châu Âu – Trung Đông vẫn giảm lần lượt 
14.1% và 16.1% yoy trong tháng 7/2026 (tháng thứ 5 liên tiếp), trái ngược với 
mức tăng 3.9% của thị trường hàng hóa hàng không toàn cầu. Diễn biến này 
phần nào giải thích việc sản lượng hàng quốc tế của SCS tiếp tục giảm khoảng 
10% yoy trong tháng 7–8, dù tần suất bay của các hãng Trung Đông đã phục 
hồi đáng kể.

Chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng hàng quốc tế và nội địa trong bốn tháng cuối 
năm lần lượt giảm 4% và 2% yoy, cả năm 2026 đạt 209.4 nghìn tấn (-7% yoy) và 
52.2 nghìn tấn (-4% yoy). Doanh thu khai thác nhà ga được kỳ vọng tiếp tục 
được hỗ trợ bởi việc điều chỉnh tăng giá dịch vụ từ đầu năm, bù đắp một phần 
tác động từ sản lượng suy giảm.

Biểu đồ 6. Mức độ phục hồi chuyến bay của các hãng hàng không vùng Vịnh 
Đến ngày 21/09/2026, tần suất bay của các 
hãng vùng Vịnh đã phục hồi đáng kể, phần 
lớn đạt khoảng 85–100% mức trước xung đột, 
riêng Qatar Airways đạt 97%, tăng từ mức 
87% ghi nhận giữa tháng 6.

### Page 5

Long Thành tạo áp lực lên sản 
lượng và biên lợi nhuận từ 2027, 
triển vọng dài hạn phụ thuộc khả 
năng tham gia vận hành nhà ga mới 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng nhu cầu vận tải hàng hóa hàng không khu vực phía Nam duy 
trì tăng trưởng trong trung hạn, với sản lượng hàng quốc tế/nội địa tăng 8%/4% 
yoy năm 2027 và 9%/6% mỗi năm từ 2028, được hỗ trợ bởi hoạt động xuất 
nhập khẩu, thương mại điện tử và sự phát triển của chuỗi cung ứng. Tuy nhiên, 
sản lượng hàng quốc tế qua SCS dự kiến chịu áp lực khi các chuyến bay từng 
bước chuyển sang Long Thành. Trong kịch bản cơ sở, chúng tôi giả định tỷ lệ 
chuyến bay quốc tế chuyển sang Long Thành lần lượt đạt 10%/35%/55%/70% 
trong giai đoạn 2027–2030, với tác động lên sản lượng thực tế phụ thuộc vào 
tiến độ khai thác nhà ga hàng hóa và hệ thống logistics tại sân bay mới.

Đồng thời, giá dịch vụ quốc tế dự kiến bắt đầu giảm từ giữa năm 2027 nhằm giữ 
chân các hãng hàng không, trong khi giá nội địa kỳ vọng duy trì tăng 3%/năm. 
Theo đó, biên lợi nhuận gộp dự kiến giảm nhẹ trong năm 2027 và chịu áp lực 
đáng kể từ 2028 khi tỷ trọng hàng quốc tế có mức giá dịch vụ cao suy giảm.

Bên cạnh đó, SCS có cơ hội mở rộng quy mô khai thác trong dài hạn thông qua 
việc tham gia vận hành nhà ga hàng hóa số 1 tại Long Thành, công suất thiết kế 
550 nghìn tấn/năm. Doanh nghiệp đã ký biên bản ghi nhớ với ACV về khả năng 
tham gia vận hành nhà ga này, qua đó có thể tiếp cận nguồn hàng dịch chuyển 
từ Tân Sơn Nhất nếu được lựa chọn. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi chưa đưa nguồn thu 
từ dự án vào kịch bản cơ sở khi chưa có kết quả lựa chọn đơn vị vận hành chính 
thức.

Biểu đồ 7. Thông lượng hàng và biên LNG của SCS 2022 – 2028F

Nguồn: SCS, KBSV dự phóng

Nền tảng tài chính lành mạnh và 
chính sách cổ tức tiền mặt ổn định 
hỗ trợ định giá cổ phiếu 
 Chúng tôi kỳ vọng SCS tiếp tục duy trì chính sách cổ tức tiền mặt ổn định trong 
những năm tới nhờ khả năng tạo dòng tiền cao, quy mô tiền gửi lớn và mức 
đòn bẩy tài chính thấp. Trong 1H2026, thu nhập tài chính tăng mạnh đã bù đắp 
phần lớn sự suy giảm lợi nhuận từ hoạt động khai thác nhà ga, giúp LNST duy 
trì ổn định ở mức 361 tỷ VND (+0.5% yoy). Chúng tôi dự phóng cổ tức tiền mặt 
năm 2026 tiếp tục đạt 5,000 VND/cổ phiếu, trước khi duy trì ở mức 3,000 
VND/cổ phiếu/năm từ 2027, phù hợp với triển vọng lợi nhuận chịu áp lực từ 
Long Thành và nhu cầu tích lũy nguồn lực tài chính để tham gia các dự án mới.

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 50,700 VND/cổ phiếu  
 Sử dụng phương pháp chiết khấu dòng tiền (DCF), trên cơ sở cập nhật triển 
vọng kinh doanh và tác động từ quá trình chuyển đổi khai thác sang Long 
Thành, chúng tôi đưa ra giá mục tiêu cho SCS là 50,700 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương với mức tiềm năng sinh lời 14.3% so với mức giá đóng cửa ngày 
22/09/2026. Trong kịch bản cơ sở, chúng tôi chưa đưa nguồn thu từ việc tham 
gia vận hành nhà ga hàng hóa số 1 tại Long Thành vào định giá. Các giả định 
chính bao gồm: 
- Thông lượng hàng quốc tế năm 2026 đạt 209.4 nghìn tấn (-7% yoy), với 
bốn tháng cuối năm giảm 4% yoy do mạng bay Trung Đông chưa phục 
hồi hoàn toàn. Nhu cầu hàng quốc tế khu vực phía nam dự kiến tăng 
8% năm 2027 và 9%/năm trong giai đoạn 2028–2030, kéo theo lượng 
hàng qua SCS trong 2027/2028/2029/2030 sẽ giảm lần lượt -2.8%/-
21%/-25%/-27% yoy, trước khi tăng trưởng trở lại từ 2031 sau khi đạt 
tỷ trọng phân bổ tối ưu giữa SGN và LTA. 
- Thông lượng hàng quốc nội năm 2026 đạt 52.2 nghìn tấn (-4% yoy), với 
bốn tháng cuối năm giảm 2% yoy do áp lực chi phí vận tải và điều 
chỉnh mạng bay. Sản lượng dự kiến phục hồi 4% năm 2027 và 6%/năm 
từ 2028 khi chi phí nhiên liệu hạ nhiệt và năng lực khai thác cải thiện. 
- Giá nội địa dự kiến tăng 3%/năm, trong khi giá quốc tế bắt đầu giảm 
từ giữa 2027 nhằm giữ chân các hãng hàng không. Biên lợi nhuận gộp 
dự kiến giảm nhẹ trong 2027 và chịu áp lực đáng kể từ 2028 khi tỷ 
trọng hàng quốc tế suy giảm và chi phí cố định không giảm tương ứng

Document 302: Fable 5.5要来了？报道称Anthropic正开发新模型，主打_意图推断_能力

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fable 5.5要来了？报道称Anthropic正开发新模型，主打_意图推断_能力.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

据 The Information 报道， Anthropic 正开发⼀款能主动推断⽤⼾模糊指令真实意图的新模
型，具备更强推理与创造性问题解决能⼒。分析认为若模糊指令推理能⼒落地，⽤⼾与 AI
助⼿的交互⽅式将改变，⽤⼾不再需要精⼼设计提⽰词，模型将承担更多 " 揣摩意图 " 的⼯
作。
摘要：
Anthropic 正在开发⼀款能够主动推断⽤⼾模糊指令背后真实意图的新模型， AI 助⼿的产品竞争
焦点，正从 " 听懂并执⾏指令 " 转向 " 理解意图 " 。
10 ⽉ 19 ⽇，据 The Information 报道，⼀位使⽤过该模型的⼈⼠透露，这款尚未发布的模型在⽤
⼾指令含糊不清时，仍能推断出⽤⼾试图完成的⽬标，并展现出更强的推理能⼒和更具创造性
的问题解决能⼒。
报道未披露模型名称、定价或上下⽂窗⼝等技术参数。同⽇， The Information 联合创始⼈ Amir
Efrati 在 X 平台发⽂称：
截⾄ 10 ⽉ 20 ⽇， Anthropic 官⽅模型⻚⾯仍仅列出四款现役模型： Claude Fable 5.1 、 Claude

Opus 5.5 、 Claude Sonnet 5.5 与 Claude Haiku 5.5 。 Anthropic ⾃ 10 ⽉ 7 ⽇发布 Haiku 5.5 以来，
未再发布任何模型公告。
模糊指令下的 " 意图推断 "
当前主流⼤模型⾯对模糊指令，通常倾向于要求⽤⼾澄清或给出保守的通⽤回答。
Anthropic 新模型的关键差异在于，它在指令不完整、不精确时，主动推断⽤⼾最可能的真实⽬
标并据此⾏动。据报道援引⼀位使⽤过该模型的⼈⼠描述，该模型具备更强的推理能⼒和更具
创造性的问题解决能⼒。
若模糊指令推理能⼒落地，⽤⼾与 AI 助⼿的交互⽅式将改变：⽤⼾不再需要精⼼设计提⽰词，
模型将承担更多 " 揣摩意图 " 的⼯作。
在编程、数据分析等复杂任务场景中，⽤⼾的初始需求往往并不完整，⼀款能推断意图的模型
可以在⽤⼾只给出粗略⽬标时，⾃主拆解任务、补⻬缺失信息并交付结果。
新模型的发布时间表与落地节奏尚未公布，其能否真正改写  AI 助⼿的交互范式仍有待观察。
Anthropic 是否即将发布 Fable 5.5 ？看起来像 ……

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

步升级，推动  AI 编程代理向全⾃动代码⽣成演进。

► 伊朗支持的葉門胡塞武裝組織持續攻擊沙烏
地阿 拉 伯 和荷 姆 茲 海峽 船 隻，美 期 中 選舉
前，地緣政治風險不減 ，添股市賣壓 。中國
長鑫存儲在新型晶片研發取得進展 ，衝擊半
導體類股 ，美光與三星 、海力士記憶體廠即
使仍供不應求 ，亦遭市場拋售 ，南韓股指週
跌幅逾 18% 。 網 通 晶 片 大 廠 博 通 執 行 長 表

示：資金是 AI建設擴張面臨的最大瓶頸 ，光
通訊股領跌走弱，科技、半導體股重挫。
► 美股第三季財報登場 ，花旗、高盛本季淨利
優於預期 ，但富國銀行淨利息收入失色 ，摩
根大通自 2023年4Q以來 ，首次獲利不如預
期，美銀亦不佳 。艾斯摩爾上修全年營收展
望，透露部分客戶已洽詢2029年至2030年設
備訂單，顯示晶片擴產需求仍強 。台積電3Q
毛利率達66.4%較前月略減，因2奈米快速放
量與海外廠產能利用率造成稀釋 ，但今年資
本支出財測維持600到640億美元不變，反映
代理AI客戶需求強勁 ，預期2026年至2028年
資本支出將較過去三年進一步顯著提高。

資產同步下挫。

► 美國9月CPI年增率上升至 3.7%，核心CPI年
增率由 2.4%升至2.6%，核心月增率 0.3%，
高於預期值0.2%。能源是通膨反彈的主因 ，
貢獻整體 CPI月增幅超過一半；能源衝擊雖
為主導 ，但消費韌性使企業保有成本轉嫁能
力，住房與部分服務價格維持黏性 ，需求面
通膨略有提高 ，加上企業資本支出提升經濟
與企業獲利韌性，使總需求不易快速降溫。
► 凱投預期Fed 12月將升息1碼，明年3月再升
1碼，政策利率可能在 4%以上維持更久 。受
到油價高盤 、通膨僵固 ，與財政惡化 、政府
發債籌資逢企業債搶錢等影響 ，美歐日主要
國家公 債利 率近 期 來到高 位 。其中 ， 法國
2027年財政預算欲刪減支出(包括養老金、公
部門工資等)引發民眾反彈 ，衝擊主權信用評
等，法國CDS與法德10年期公債利差擴大 、
歐元走弱 ，美元轉強 ，商品、黃金承壓 。法
國政治 不確定性讓 歐股修正 、法股最疲 ，成
熟國家公債殖利率升高 ，增加企業借貸成本

與股市評價壓力，續為金融市場擾動因子。

資料來源：Bloomberg，Factset，Goldman Sachs，凱基證券整理，註：右上圖之大型雲端服務供應商(CSP)企業為Amazon、Alphabet、Microsoft、Oracle
► 美國第三季財報季展開 ，市場將觀察企業在
高利率環境下的獲利表現 。根據FactSet數據
顯示 ，標普500指數第三季獲利 成長預估達
29.5%，顯示企業基本面仍具支撐 ，其中科
技 產業 受 惠AI 需求帶動 ， 獲 利 成 長預 估達
65%，持續扮演企業獲利的重要成長動能。
► 聚焦科技類股 ，AI資本支出仍為本輪企業獲
利成長的主要驅動力 。高盛指出 ，第三季AI

基礎建設相關企業將貢獻標普 500獲利增長
的54%。此外，大型CSP之雲端業務營收有
望加速成長 ，反映AI運算及雲端服務需求持
續提升，有助支撐後續資本支出擴張動能。
► 近期市場波動仍受高利率環境影響 ，壓抑投
資人風險偏好與估值擴張空間 ，顯示市場目
前壓力更多來自評價面而非基本面 。隨財報
數據陸續公布 ，若企業持續繳出優於預期的
獲利表現 ，並維持正向財測 ，市場有望逐步
回歸基本面評價 ，使企業獲利成長逐步支撐
股市表現。
美企財報獲利穩健，對股市中長期有撐

美股第三季獲利成長持續上修，科技為主要支撐 CSP雲端營收加速，資本支出變現跡象持續受驗證
評價面疑慮下，股價難以進一步擴張

投資焦點
► 醫療保健 、醫療服務 與生物技術等行業 ，在
景氣不好時 ，市場預期民眾對醫療保健產業
的需求 不易 下降 ， 成為傳 統防禦 型類 股代
表。目前金融環境利率偏高 ，11月美國總統
大選國會恐變天 ，股市波動風險上升 ，觀察
過去當 VIX恐慌指數上揚時 ，醫療保健產業
相較於科技股更穩健，具有更好抗震能力。
► 醫療與生技類股近一年漲幅明顯 ，主因1)生
技股過去一段時間受到高利率 、融資成本及
臨床試驗風險壓抑 ，部分公司估值偏低 。當
臨床數據改善 、或導入AI篩選資料與 藥物，
投資人重新評價未來現金流與價值 。2)大型
藥廠面臨既有藥品專利到期壓力 ，透過收購
取得新藥與研發管線 ，提高有潛力的生技公
司併購價值 ，帶動整體產業估值回升 。3)肥
胖、癌症與慢性病用藥創造長期市場 需求，
創新藥物 (如瘦瘦針 )讓部分醫療公司成長不
再只依賴人口老化，高盛今年7月將全球減肥
藥市場 2030年銷售額預估上調 15%至1,140
億美元，看好此類藥品的長期商業潛力。

醫療生技近年表現佳，估今年3Q醫院客流量年增4%VIX恐慌指數上升時，健護醫療表現相較穩健
市場波動上升、醫療保健表現佳，獲利穩定成長
投資焦點

### Page 8

8
內部參考文件請勿外流
► 美國醫療費用深受法令影響 ，由於商業雇主
贊助保險 (ESI)和平價醫療法案 (ACA)的自付
額增加 ，以及最高自付費用要求 ，較去年同
期提高 ，使今年第三季美國整體住院人數 、
手術量和醫院客流量均呈現改善 。高盛預估
今年第三季醫院客流量年增4%，加上季節性
因素，下半年醫療保健利用率將較上半年成
長，整體第四季預估獲利穩健 ，甚至可延至
明年，反應在近期XLV ETF股價創新高。
► 展望2027年，各醫療保險計畫普遍優先考慮
透過提高會員保費 ，和要求費用分攤(尤其是
提高會員自付費用上限 )來擴大利潤空間 ，並
持續優化覆蓋範圍和產品組合 ，預估商業雇
主提供的醫療保險 (ESI)保費趨勢，將在明年
加速成長至過去 20 年來的最高水準 。 以
Refinitiv預估明年美國各產業獲利成長來看，
醫療保健和生技相較於今年獲利明顯增加 ，
反而今年獲利極佳的能源和通訊受高基期影
響，獲利增速下滑 。投資人在整體金融環境
波動放大時期，宜以醫療生技股穩中求利。

投資焦點

股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長 ，市場靜待財報結果與展望 ，和對AI資本支出回收影響 。
考量地緣衝突 、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉 ，短期金融市場紛擾不止 ，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度 。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變 ，逢股市拉回時可分批布局 ，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間 ，殖利率偏高環境使 短中期債券提供吸
引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利
差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金

### Page 17

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

### Page 4

Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.

### Page 6

6
When cyber weakness = share price consequence…
Investors are more aware of cyber risk today, but they rarely sell a stock due to
perceived cyber vulnerabilities (although we are aware of some who have).
—What is the potential impact? Our dbDataInsights event study of 47 breaches
shows a cumulative breach-attributed return of c. -2.9% within the first two
trading days and c. -6.0% through first 20 days trading days. The erosion then
continues more gradually, reaching c. -6.7% nine months after disclosure (see

slide 13).
Which industries are most vulnerable?
—Our survey respondents point to (1) government & public sector (52%), (2)
financial services (50%), (3) energy and utilities (46%) and (4) healthcare and life
sciences follow (39%, rising to 48% among US respondents).
We should caveat, that high sector exposure does not mean every company in it is
equally vulnerable. Some firms in these “exposed” sectors may very well be better
prepared given they have built specialist teams and defence protocols for decades.

What about access to credit and ratings? Credit and rating providers are paying
attention. Moody’s and S&P both cut their outlook on Jaguar Land Rover within two
months of its 2025 hack. Today, this type of impact is not widespread. But if risk
escalates through a systemic incident or an AI-driven step change in attacks these
mechanisms, including ESG ratings, could reprice asset ownership or impact access
to credit. In fact, some lenders are already pricing it. Journal of Corporate Finance

research using US syndicated loan data found borrowers with persistently higher cyber
risk pay 4–13bp more.
The bottom line? While a broader systemic attack is hard to prepare for, corporates
and investors should be asking questions about vulnerabilities, security investment,
preparedness and recovery should an attack occur. This is especially true for
industries quickly adopting AI capabilities and / or a high threat landscape.

dbDataInsights Cybersecurity Expert Survey methodology
—This survey was conducted among 54 cybersecurity professionals across the US
and Europe in September of 2026. Questions took the form of either multiple

choice selections or free form responses. The dbDataInsights team received the
responses anonymously, but respondents were required to work or consult in
cyber security roles
—Who took the survey? On self-identified roles, around 80% sit inside an
organisation’s cybersecurity function, with the remainder working independently
(15%) or in academia and research (6%). The most common titles were security
architect (23%), Chief Information Security Officer (16%) and security analyst (9%),

alongside Head of Security, Deputy CISO, Incident Response Lead and Security
Operations Lead.
—Only a small minority of respondents work for cyber resellers, thus survey views
are primarily attributable to those working within an organization on
cybersecurity.
—Survey participants were also asked for their perspectives on cyber resilience and
governance, what is misunderstood, alongside emerging threats, risk to AI
adoption and the influence of geopolitics.

—While responses were anonymous, in some cases we conducted follow up
interviews with certain participants where contact details were made available.
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 10

10
Risk from AI-enabled attacks and added AI adoption vulnerabilities
Insider threats and supply chain compromises remain a growing concern
“The fear of AI writing undetectable malware is … overblown. Attackers were
never short of malware, since they can buy it ready made along with initial
access. Good detection looks at behaviour rather than exact code, so a rewritten
payload still has to do the same suspicious things.”
“Frontier AI models are currently the main hype. Automated phishing and

scams are underappreciated, yet they are responsible for the bigger
monetary losses at the moment.”
“There are too many organisations relying on the same providers for
critical services and this means single points of failure.”
“A corporation might be very secure but if they rely on a third party that
does not have the same level of security posture, [it] could be
compromised via this third-party (basically their weak link).”
“Existing employee login behaviour and usage of AI is underappreciated –

it’s what poses the biggest threat to a breach.”
“No company can guarantee they won’t be breached, so there needs to
be more focus put on the ability to recover and survive an attack.”
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research .

### Page 11

11
Experts believe governments are not keeping pace, and no one owns AI oversight
How experts surveyed by dbDataInsights view governance and regulation
—AI adoption is outpacing internal controls. As one expert put it: “Companies
are pushing the use of AI more to their employees without the proper
mechanisms and policies in place to prevent misuse or attacks initiated by the AI
systems themselves.”
—Regulation is struggling to keep pace. 81% of respondents agree that

cybersecurity-related laws and regulations are failing to keep up with
technological change, and 54% say regulatory requirements can improve security
but may also slow innovation. One respondent highlighted “the levels of
uncertainty, the long running nature of the problems, and the rapidity of change.”
—Responsibility for AI oversight remains unsettled. International bodies are the
leading choice, supported by 50% of respondents. No option commands a

majority, underscoring the lack of consensus on who should oversee AI
development.
—Better board questions may not guarantee better decisions. The survey
points to more informed questioning by corporate leadership, yet 65% doubt that
leadership teams understand cyber risk well enough to make informed business
trade-offs. Measuring activity more effectively than outcomes further complicates
oversight.
—Accountability is becoming personal. Only 43% say accountability for cyber risk

is clearly defined in their governance framework, yet the consequences now reach
the top as a handful of CEOs have left or gone into early retirement after
financially consequential cyber breaches.
Who should primarily oversee AI development?
dbDataInsights survey – September 2026, based on overseeing development while managing cyber risk

Material cyber breaches can cause a notable drop in share price
Our dbDataInsights study found a 2.9% drop on average within two trading days, with the cumulative breach-attributed return of -6.0% on trading day 20.

Source: dbDataInsights, Bloomberg Finance LP., Deutsche Bank Research, Company filings. Event study of reported cybersecurity incidents, 2015 -2025, 34 US and 13 European companies across nine sectors.
What typically happens to a company’s shares when a breach is reported?
—Short-term impact: The market prices the breach immediately. Our model shows
cumulative breach-attributed (i.e. isolating only the part of the share price move
we can pin on the breach itself) returns are c. -2.9% within two trading days of

at one year, although the one-year point estimate (our single best-guess figure)
should be treated with caution given the width of the confidence band.
—Pre-breach price behaviour: We find no evidence of information leakage ahead
of disclosure. While the breach component for the [-10,-1] window (ten trading
days before the news broke) is +0.04%, the corresponding t-stat value of 0.03 falls
notably below the usual bar for significance (c.2). As such, we conclude the market

had not begun reacting before the announcement.
Event study methodology
—Sample: 47 publicly reported cybersecurity incidents across the US and Europe
over 2015-2025, measured against each company’s regional sector benchmark.
Requirement of 5 years of stock returns pre-attack and 1 year of post-attack.
—Day 0: the day an incident was first publicly reported or disclosed.
—Baseline: We use five years of pre-incident share-price performance to
estimate: (i) Beta (β): how sensitive a company’s returns are to movements in its

sector and (ii) Alpha (α): the extent to which the company typically outperforms
or underperforms its sector after adjusting for that sensitivity.
—Attribution: post-incident returns are split into sector-relative returns, alpha
(α) and breach (abnormal returns attributed to the disclosure).
—95% confidence interval: Range within which we can say with 95% confidence
that the real share price impact lies, based on our sample data.
Total return attribution

Marks & Spencer (Reported Apr 2025)
What happened?
A breach via an attack on a connected 3rd
party forced the suspension of all online
orders and led to empty store shelves.
What did it cost?
~£136mn of direct costs related to the
attack, estimated group operating profit
impact of around £300mn for the FY. M&S
also said they had received £100mn via
cyber insurance.
Coupang (Reported Nov 2025)
What happened?
A data breach exposed the records of
more than 30mn users with a former

employee reportedly behind the attack.
What did it cost?
The firm was fined more than ~625bn won
($409mn) (2/3rd directly attributable to
data leak), and also announced a $1.2bn
voucher compensation scheme.
UnitedHealth (Reported Feb 2024)
What happened?
Hackers reportedly used stolen credentials
to access a subsidiary’s (Change
Healthcare) systems, stealing data and
deploying ransomware.
What did it cost?
Total costs exceeded $3bn, with the CEO
summoned to testify before Congress,

where he confirmed that a $22mn ransom
was paid.

What happened?
A data breach saw hackers expose
sensitive customer information, including
145.5mn Social Security numbers and
more than 200k credit card numbers.
Attackers reportedly moved inside Equifax
systems for 76 days before being detected.
What did it cost?
Settlements, fines and legal costs
exceeded $1bn, while the firm also spent
more than $1.5bn on tech upgrades

following the incident.

Nike (Reported Jan 2026)
What happened?
A cybercrime group leaked what it claimed
was 1.4TB of internal Nike data (appearing
to cover metrics such as product

of a ransom deadline.
What did it cost?
While Nike did confirm it was investigating

shown in the chart does not appear to be
related to the breach.
T-Mobile (Reported Aug 2021)
What happened?
An attacker reportedly targeted an
exposed router as an entry point, before
using brute force attacks to gain further
access to T-Mobile systems.
What did it cost?
In 2022 the firm agreed to pay $500mn to
settle a class-action lawsuit ($350mn
payment to class members  plus $150mn
on security spend) but has since suffered
further data breaches, contributing to an

additional $31.5mn regulatory settlement.
Coinbase (Reported May 2025)
What happened?
Attackers bribed at least one support
agent at a connected 3rd party to access
Coinbase support systems and steal
customer data, then tricked users into
sending crypto.
What did it cost?
Coinbase refused to pay the $20mn
ransom demanded by the attackers to
keep the attack quiet. Instead they
reimbursed affected users and cited costs
of up to $400mn. The stock impact was

short-lived, though MSCI applied an ESG
score deduction.

What happened?
Production was shut down for five weeks
due to what was deemed a ransomware
attack; though no ransom was asked for
(later reporting suggested potential Russia
links were being investigated).
What did it cost?
JLR posted £485mn Q2’ FY26 loss (vs.
£398mn profit in Q2’ FY25), with an
additional £196mn in “cyber-related costs”.
These impacts filtered through to the

share price of JLR’s Parent (Tata Motors),
while UK economic impacts were
estimated at £1.9bn.

Targets go beyond tech and can also impact critical infrastructure
MGM Resorts (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
A vishing call to the IT help desk was reportedly used to reset credentials, after which
ransomware took down hotel systems, bookings and casino floor operations for several
days.
What did it cost?
MGM declined to pay a ransom. Around $100mn hit to third-quarter 2023 results plus
roughly ~$10mn of additional one-time expenses related to the incident.

Caesars Entertainment (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
The same social-engineering group as in the MGM incident targeted an outsourced IT
support vendor, gaining access to the loyalty programme database and member data.
What did it cost?
Caesars paid roughly $15mn of a reported $30mn demand and kept customer-facing
operations running.
NHS / Synnovis (Reported Jun 2024)
What happened?
Ransomware against pathology provider Synnovis significantly reduced its capacity to

process tests leading to delays / cancellations of over 11,000 appointments.
What did it cost?
Estimated 2024 direct operational financial impact of around GBP 32mn for Synnovis,
several times the profit reported in the prior year, plus wider NHS disruption costs.
Colonial Pipeline (Reported May 2021)
What happened?
A compromised VPN credential without multi-factor authentication allowed attackers to
access the network where they subsequently deployed ransomware. The operator shut the

pipeline down, triggering fuel shortages (c. 70% of gas stations in some states ran out of
fuel) across the US East Coast.
What did it cost?
A $4.4mn ransom was paid, of which about $2.3mn was later recovered by the US
authorities.
Bitget (Reported Sep 2026)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a vulnerability in a 3rd party security product to steal internal network
credentials, forge withdrawal instructions, bypass risk controls and ultimately steal crypto.

What did it cost?
An estimated $388mn worth of funds was transferred out by the attackers. While the firm
reimbursed affected users via its User Protection Fund, there were significant outflows
following the incident as users moved to take funds off the platform.
Progress Software (Reported May 2023)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a zero-day SQL injection vulnerability in Progress Software’s MOVEit file
transfer system. This was then used by attackers to steal data from firms running the

### Page 17

Cyber breaches can impact consumer behaviour
Roughly 4 in 10 consumers we surveyed say they would stop buying from a company they shop with after a known breach or attack.
Source: dbDataInsights monthly consumer survey, Deutsche Bank Research. EU Average includes France, Germany, Italy and Spain.
How will customers respond to a data breach once it occurs?
dbDataInsights survey of consumer responses, % of respondents
How will customers react once the breach is resolved?

Consumer responses among those who would either stop buying or were unsure, % of respondents
Consumer surveys suggest that cyber breaches can undermine customer
loyalty.  Results of our dbDataInsights Consumer survey show the potential
mindset of a consumer after a cyber breach occurs:
—Across the US, UK and EU average, 39–41% say they would stop buying, while only
27–28% would continue. Around a third are unsure.
—Among those who would stop buying or are unsure, only 18–22% say they would

return as normal. Another 30–35% would buy selected products only, 17–20%
would not return, and 28–29% remain unsure. Note that this implies a range of
13-15% believe they would not shop at the store again after the breach is
resolved.
Admittedly, these stated intentions may overstate actual switching, particularly for
essential purchases where alternatives are limited. Nevertheless, the findings
suggest that cyber breaches that put customer privacy at risk can influence

purchasing decisions, especially if they have alternatives.
